People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in, I really appreciate it. I, uh... I hope I'm doing this right, and I don't want to be a bother.

Well, doctor... I'm not exactly sure what the reason is. I really try to be a good neighbor and stay out of everybody's way, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm making things up—I know how bad that probably sounds.

I know what's going on. I hope it's okay that I came to talk to you about it. Like, I know they're watching my comings and goings. And those notes they slip to the office... I'm trying to do this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs that you were completely convinced of, despite others telling you they were not true?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, sir. I really haven't. I... I know I keep coming back to the exact same thing, and I'm so sorry if I'm not giving you the kind of answers you need today. I really don't mean to be difficult or hold up your time. \n\nIt's truly just that one situation I mentioned before... about knowing the folks at my building are watching my comings and goings, and slipping those notes to the office about me. I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other beliefs that you held onto for a long time, despite others telling you they were not true?\n\nPatient: No, doctor, honestly, I haven't. I... I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record, and I'm really sorry to keep bringing it up, but it really is just that one thing. I've never been the type to get strange ideas or argue with folks about things. \n\nIt's just... well, like I said, I just know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings, and slipping those notes to the office about me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I'm really starting to worry that I'm not giving you the right answer, Doc. I feel terrible. Am I misunderstanding what you're asking? I promise I'm searching my memory, but I really haven't had any other thoughts like that. 

It's truly just the one thing I came in for... the situation with the folks at my apartment watching my comings and goings, and slipping those notes about me to the office. I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm failing your test here by saying the exact same thing again. I would tell you if there was anything else, I really, really would. Are we doing okay? I just want to make sure I'm being a good patient for you.

I haven't had anything else. \n\nI've been thinking and thinking, and it's still just the one thing. I just know, deep down, that the folks in my building are keeping tabs on when I leave and when I come back, and they're passing those notes about me to the management. Even when everyone insists they're just standard flyers on the doors... that's the only time I've ever been so sure of something when everyone else said I was wrong. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... well, if you showed me a video... I suppose I would have to look at it. I really would try to look at it with an open mind, Doctor. I know you're just trying to help me, and I want to be agreeable. 

It's just... it's so hard because I *know* they're watching my comings and goings. I know they're slipping those notes about me to the office. Even when everybody keeps telling me it's just regular notices on the doors, it feels so real to me. But if you went to all the trouble to get a video to show me... I would try my very best to believe you. I wouldn't want to argue with you, I promise. 

Would that be the right way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm working with you, not against you. I really don't want to be a difficult patient.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, dear... I really hope I'm not frustrating you, Doctor. I feel like I'm failing a test because I still don't have any other new things to tell you about. I'm so sorry. 

But... um, since you specifically asked about it being more than a year... I suppose I need to be completely honest with you. I didn't want to say the exact timeframe before because I was a little ashamed, and I didn't want you to think worse of me... but those thoughts about the neighbors watching me and reporting to the office... they've actually been with me for about a year and a month now. 

I hope it's alright that I'm just telling you how long it's been going on. I just get so embarrassed, and I want you to see that I'm doing my best to hold it together. Does that help answer your question? I really am trying to give you exactly what you need.
